### Image prompt
````text
Keep the FIRST attached image exactly as it is - the same room layout, the same camera position, distance and angle, a different woman in the same pose near the top of the same ladder with both arms up, the same inflatable Santa taped flat to the ceiling with the same silver duct tape, the same curved staircase, garland, stockings, fireplace, mirror, Christmas tree, sofa and rug positions, the same lighting, and the same clean empty band of ceiling across the top sixth of the frame. Change only these two things:

1. THE WOMAN: replace her with a mother about 32 with light olive skin and long straight dark-brown hair pulled back in a loose low ponytail. She is wearing matching Christmas American-flag pyjamas - a pyjama top with bold red and white horizontal stripes and a navy blue yoke across the shoulders covered in small white stars, and matching red and white striped pyjama bottoms, soft brushed cotton, slightly loose the way real pyjamas fit. Same body scale, same pose, same thick grey wool socks, same roll of duct tape in her left hand.

2. THE ROOM: a different double-height Gustavian Scandinavian living room, with soft white plaster walls and pale grey-white painted wooden panelling, a white-painted curved staircase in the same position, a pale grey limestone fireplace with a lit fire and a gilded oval mirror above it, an oatmeal bouclé sofa and a sage-green wool rug over whitewashed oak floorboards. Keep the high plain white ceiling at exactly the same height and shape. The garland, stockings, tree, lamps and furniture stay in exactly the same positions and at the same scale as @Image 1. A real fabric United States flag hangs flat against the plain soft white wall in the same background position as @Image 1, clearly readable stars and stripes, hanging straight and still. It does not cover the Christmas tree, the fireplace or the staircase, and it does not reach up into the empty band of ceiling at the top of the frame.

Everything else in the picture stays exactly as it is. Same neutral white balance with no orange cast, same iPhone snapshot look, tack sharp and in focus everywhere, absolutely nothing blurry, no background blur.

REFERENCE ROLES: @Image 1 is the approved Video 6 hook and owns the exact camera, low three-quarters-from-behind angle, framing, tall A-frame ladder, woman scale and raised-arm pose, taped inflatable Santa, duct tape roll, American-flag pyjamas and crisp iPhone lighting. @Image 2 supplies only the Gustavian Scandinavian material palette - soft white plaster, pale grey-white panelling, pale grey limestone, whitewashed oak, oatmeal bouclé and sage green. Do not import its children, projected Christmas imagery, dome ceiling, darkness, camera or composition. This hook contains one woman on the ladder, as in @Image 1. Santa is taped flat to the ceiling with the same criss-crossed silver tape. No projected patterns, no text, no captions, no watermark, no bokeh. Perfectly exposed, extremely high quality, tack sharp edge to edge.
````

### Video prompt - take 1
**Clip:** `clips/v18_c01_g1.mp4` — take 1, generated 2026-09-24, unreviewed.
**Settings:** seedance_2_5 · mode omni_reference · 1080p · 4 s · 9:16 · audio on · declined_preset_id 24bae836-2c4a-48e0-89b6-49fcc0b21612
**Job ID:** `63ad8bd0-75e0-439a-b65d-5b5635cee7af`
**Attached:**
- start_image: `starting_frames/v18_c01_g1.png` · Higgsfield media `7cc3af15-5d94-4727-abe4-71f363687647`
**Result:** https://d8j0ntlcm91z4.cloudfront.net/user_REMOVED/hf_20260923_223603_63ad8bd0-75e0-439a-b65d-5b5635cee7af.mp4 · md5 `e101685d69d88800edf48c778493da9e` (downloaded byte-identical) · 1080×1920, 24 fps, 4.05 s, no cut detected.
**Lineage:** Reused byte-identical from v16 C01 (clips/v16_c01_g1.mp4, verified record, IN THE CUT - hook 11). No noun needed changing (the prompt names only the woman, the ladder, the rug and the inflatable Santa). Same model, 1080p, 4 s, 9:16.
**Note:** the clip's first frame is closer in than the approved start frame (her back and the Santa fill the frame) and the camera then tilts down with the fall, although the prompt forbids tilting.

````text
Real home video, shot on a hand-held iPhone by a person standing in the room a few steps behind her, phone held upright at chest height. One continuous shot. The camera stays in exactly the same spot for the whole clip with only tiny natural hand sway and micro-jitter - the small live breathing wobble of real hand-held phone footage, never a steady locked-off tripod look. It does not reframe, does not follow her, does not tilt, pan, zoom or push in. No phone, hand, fingers, thumb or arm ever appears in the foreground.

HARD RULE, THIS ONE FIRST: the whole clip is ONE single unbroken shot from the first frame to the last. No cut, no jump cut, no second angle, no scene change, no transition, no dissolve at any point. The motion runs continuously - every moment grows out of the moment before it, and her body never resets, jumps position or teleports between one part and the next.

For the first second she is just taping the inflatable Santa to the ceiling - both hands up, pressing and smoothing, nothing dramatic.

Then, without any pause or break in the movement, she loses her footing and falls off the ladder to the floor. Her arms are still up and still coming down out of the taping motion as her balance goes - the taping and the fall are one unbroken piece of movement, not two separate actions.

The fall has real weight. An ordinary woman's balance goes, gravity takes her, and she is on the floor in well under a second - fast, heavy, a bit ugly, exactly like real footage of someone coming off a ladder. Her limbs are heavy and human, not floaty or rubbery, and she never hangs in the air. The ladder goes over with her and clatters onto the rug.

She lands hard and the movement carries straight on into stillness without a break - she settles, rocks slightly as her weight comes to rest, and stays down on the floor for the rest of the clip, not moving much, just stunned and breathing. Her chest is still moving and she is still there when the clip ends.

The inflatable Santa stays taped to the ceiling the whole time.

No slow motion. No speed ramp, no speed change of any kind - the clip plays at one constant real-time speed from start to finish. No zoom, no push-in, no pan, no tilt. No captions or text.

Audio: room tone only, no music - the tape, a short gasp, the ladder hitting the floor, then her breathing.
````

### Image prompt
````text
Copy the FIRST attached reference photo EXACTLY: same camera position, same top-down-ish angle, same distance, same vertical 9:16 framing, same table surface at the same height and same angle, same two hands resting on the box in the same position and same grip, same arms entering frame the same way. Keep OUR product box exactly as it appears in the FIRST reference and in the SECOND attached reference: a tall upright rectangular tuck-top carton, square footprint, roughly 15 x 15 x 24 cm, forest green body, dark red band across the top with a gold THEXMASGLOW wordmark, cream serif headline CHRISTMAS LIGHT PROJECTOR, a gold star-and-tree burst over a printed render of a smooth white dome-topped cylindrical projector on a separate white four-legged splayed stand base, slim red remote printed beside it, gold village skyline along the bottom, no transparent window. Keep the box at the same position, scale and angle it occupies in the FIRST reference. Change only three things.

FIRST - THE HANDS. A woman's hands, about 31, with light beige skin with warm golden undertones and slender fingers, slender fingers, narrow wrists, smooth bare skin, short clean natural nails with no polish, no jewellery of any kind - no rings, no bangle, no watch - the cuffs of a midnight-navy cable-knit wool sweater pushed back at both wrists. Same position on the box, same grip, same arms entering the frame the same way, same scale.

SECOND - THE TABLE. The surface under the box is now a solid cherry-wood dining table with warm reddish-brown flowing grain, a satin-matte finish and a softly rounded edge. Same height, same angle, same amount of the surface visible as in the FIRST reference.

THIRD - THE BACKGROUND. Behind and above the table, a festive Gustavian Scandinavian dining room with pale dove-grey painted wall panelling, a tall white-tiled Swedish corner stove hung with red knitted stockings, a gilded oval mirror, pale grey painted built-in shelves and a Christmas tree dressed with woven straw stars, small red apples and warm white fairy lights. Every background detail stays in focus; the box remains dominant through its scale and placement.

Dress the dining table more festively: a sprig of fir and a few red berries, two or three small silver and ruby-red glass baubles, a short cream candle, a soft warm string light trailing across the matte table surface, all at the edges of frame so the box stays the clear subject.

Shot on an iPhone 16 Pro, photorealistic, extremely high quality, tack-sharp focus on the box and the hands, crisp legible detail in the printing on the carton, perfect natural realistic lighting, perfectly exposed, hyper realistic, zero motion blur, nothing blurry or soft, no CGI look. Normal wide-angle lens, straight vertical lines, no fisheye distortion.

The wordmark printed on the red band must read exactly THEXMASGLOW - eleven letters, T-H-E-X-M-A-S-G-L-O-W, correctly spelled, sharp and legible, with no invented or misspelled brand name anywhere on the carton.

No captions, no watermarks, no added lettering anywhere in the image other than the printing on our own carton.

REFERENCE ROLES: @Image 1 owns the exact layout, product geometry, hand positions and camera. @Image 2 owns the established photographic look and carton artwork only; use the new hands, materials and background specified above. All parts of this photograph, including the background, are in crisp natural iPhone deep focus, no portrait mode, no bokeh, no blur.
````

````text
STARTING-FRAME AND PRINT LOCK - use the attached starting image exactly at frame 0, including the front-facing THEXMASGLOW carton. Keep the dark-red brand band, cream CHRISTMAS LIGHT PROJECTOR lettering, printed white projector and red remote, gold star/tree and gold village artwork visible and unchanged on that same rigid front panel throughout. The box stays upright and never rotates away. The left hand braces its lower side without covering the main artwork. Opening only bends the top flaps; it never erases the front printing.

AUDIO RULE - HIGHEST PRIORITY, READ THIS FIRST: this clip has NO MUSIC OF ANY KIND. No background song, no soundtrack, no score, no melody, no instruments, no singing, no humming, no jingle, no Christmas song, no festive music bed, no ambient musical pad, no strings, no piano, no bells used as music. The audio track contains ONLY real natural sound recorded in that room. No video reference is attached. If any music appears anywhere in the 4 seconds the clip is wrong.

HAND ROLES - SECOND MOST IMPORTANT: THE RIGHT HAND opens the lid and then grips and lifts the projector alone. THE LEFT HAND stays braced against the lower left side of THE BOX from first frame to last, fingers resting below the main printed artwork. The left hand never lifts, follows the projector, gestures, reaches into the box or leaves the carton. It steadies the box on the table while the right hand and product rise. Exactly two hands with separate jobs: right hand carries the product, left hand anchors the carton.

EXIT RULE - THIRD MOST IMPORTANT: the projector does NOT stay in frame at the end. THE RIGHT HAND lifts it out and keeps going straight up until the projector and right hand leave the top of frame. At 4.0s the open carton remains on the table, held steady by THE LEFT HAND. The right hand never pauses to present the product.

SPEED RULE - FOURTH MOST IMPORTANT: the hands work at a brisk, confident, real-time pace. This is a normal person opening a box at normal speed, not a careful slow-motion demonstration. The lift is QUICK and decisive. Do not slow anything down.

TASK: image-to-video. The start image is frame 0.0s. Hold its table, box artwork, props and lighting exactly.

[1] SUMMARY
First-person POV: two hands open a tall dark-green Christmas carton on a solid cherry-wood dining table with warm reddish-brown flowing grain, a satin-matte finish and a softly rounded edge, lift the white projector briskly straight up out of it and carry it out of the top of frame. Locked POV looking down. 4 seconds, one take.

[2] GLOBAL STYLE
Real handheld phone POV, warm domestic Christmas light, crisp and sharp, correctly exposed. Very slight handheld drift. Real-time speed throughout. No cinematic grade, no grain, no slow motion, no speed ramp.

[3] ASSET ROLES
The start image AND @Image 1 are the same approved image, attached as start_image and image reference. They alone control frame 0.0s, the exact printed box, hands, table, room, props and light. Begin on that exact view and animate it. No reference video, no alternate carton, no plain-box substitute.

[4] OBJECTS
THE BOX - a dark-green tuck-top carton standing upright, front panel facing camera, a dark red band across the top with a gold THEXMASGLOW wordmark, the cream serif words CHRISTMAS LIGHT PROJECTOR, a gold star-and-tree burst over a printed white dome-topped projector on a white four-legged splayed stand, a gold village skyline along the bottom, no transparent window. About 15 x 15 x 24 cm. Plain interior, no insert, no foam tray.
THE PROJECTOR - inside THE BOX: a matte-white dome-shaped body on a separate white four-legged splayed stand. Set into the top of the dome is a flat circular BLACK perforated mesh disc. The top face is BLACK and stays black for every frame. No front lens, no camera head, no swivel.
THE HANDS - exactly two hands, a woman's, with the cuff of a midnight-navy cable-knit wool sweater pushed back at each wrist and no jewellery of any kind - no rings, no bangle, no watch.

[5] LOCATION - a solid cherry-wood dining table with warm reddish-brown flowing grain, a satin-matte finish and a softly rounded edge fills the frame. The lit cream candle, fir sprigs, red berries, silver and ruby-red glass baubles and warm string lights stay exactly where they are in the start image. SHARPNESS - this frame must be tack sharp edge to edge: every moulding edge and panel corner in the door, the machined edge and the patina of the brass knob, the individual needles of the pine wreath and the woven nap of the red velvet bow, the skin texture and fine hairs on the forearm, the grain of the floorboards. Whitewashed oak floorboards, a large oatmeal bouclé sectional sofa, a round oatmeal upholstered ottoman and a soft sage-green wool rug. The side opening on the right wall remains lateral to the route, never an extra threshold ahead. a girl of about five with light-brown skin and dark curly hair in two puffs tied with green velvet ribbons; a boy of about seven with fair skin, light freckles and tousled strawberry-blond curly hair. Both fully dressed in soft sky-blue brushed-cotton Christmas pyjama sets patterned with small white polar bears in red scarves, green fir trees and silver snowflakes, with matching loose pyjama trousers and white wool socks. Preserve these identities and architecture from @Image 2.
````

````text
PROJECTED SCENE CONTENT - LOW ALONG THE WALLS, a magical CHRISTMAS SKATING POND VILLAGE: a wide frozen pond glitters under golden lantern light while children in red scarves and bobble hats skate hand in hand, a tall decorated Christmas tree glows on a small snowy island at its centre, snowmen in red scarves line the banks, horse-drawn sleighs with brass bells pass cosy half-timbered cottages with warmly lit windows and thick snowy roofs, and tall snow-laden fir trees rise between them. Along the upper walls, strings of glowing golden lanterns, red velvet bows and dense emerald garlands link the cottage roofs into a continuous Christmas panorama. ACROSS THE CEILING, a great ring of emerald and rose northern-lights ribbons circles a huge glowing full moon, and Santa's red sleigh and eight reindeer cross the face of the moon in gold, their long curling red-and-gold ribbon trail woven among crisp snowflakes and tiny silver stars. Unmistakably Christmas Eve, glowing gold, cranberry red and icy sapphire blue dominant with evergreen accents against a near-black midnight sky. One dense continuous panorama of projected light across the architecture, no isolated sticker icons, no carnival, no galaxy, no vortex. No projected shape floats physically in the room. 

CURRENT BRIEF — STRONG CONTINUOUS MOTION: The existing skaters glide steadily across the illustrated frozen pond on the walls, holding hands, swaying and turning; the horse-drawn sleighs trot along the snowy banks with the horses' legs moving and their brass bells swinging; the snowmen's red scarves flutter and the golden lanterns flicker. Preserve the artwork already visible. Every illustrated chimney emits fresh curling smoke continuously, moving at least one chimney height per second; the sleigh crosses a substantial part of the ceiling with galloping reindeer. Existing ribbon light fields carry clearly travelling aurora-like waves along their length, with brightness crests moving rather than blinking in place. The existing northern-lights ring on the ceiling is ALWAYS in motion: its emerald and rose curtains ripple, fold and travel along their length like a real aurora, shimmering continuously from the first frame to the last, never a still painted band. The cottage structures and the full moon stay anchored while characters, vehicles, smoke and light waves travel independently. The only physical motion in the room is the specified children and camera; all Christmas artwork and illustrated smoke remain flat light on real wall/ceiling surfaces. No floating 3D objects. No frozen opening or ending. Use the exact architecture and artwork in this shot's references; animate only illustrated characters actually present, never invent source-template deer or rabbits. THE PICTURE AS A WHOLE IS ANCHORED. It does not rotate, spin, swing, sweep sideways or slide across the room as one sheet. Its outer boundary stays where it is. The motion lives INSIDE it, element by element:
````
````text
- THE CHIMNEY SMOKE DRAWINGS - animated flat smoke curls rise within the projected cottage drawings on the wall, leaning right and changing shape while new drawn curls form below. These are pixels on plaster, not real smoke or haze in the room.
- THE RISING SPARK DRAWINGS - flat gold pixels climb along the wall from the illustrated village chimneys toward the painted star field at roughly one roof height each half-second. They are part of the projected wall movie, with no real sparks, airborne trails or particles between the camera and the wall.
- THE NORTHERN LIGHTS AND RIBBONS - each emerald and rose northern-lights curtain in the ring around the full moon, and each red-and-gold ribbon ripples ALONG ITS OWN LENGTH, a slow travelling wave running down the band away from the moon, about half of the ribbon's length every second, the ribbon swelling brighter and thinner as the wave passes and its lower fringe combing and re-forming. Each ribbon's overall fan stays where it is on the plaster; the wave inside it travels. Every ribbon on the ceiling is doing this at the same time.
- THE SLEIGH AND THE REINDEER - they are FLYING. The team travels steadily across the ceiling on a long sweeping curve away from where it sits in the start image, arcing in toward the full moon, covering about a quarter of the ceiling's width every second, so by 5.0s it has crossed most of the way. The reindeer's legs gallop, their heads nod, the sleigh rocks gently behind them, the long tapering trail streams out behind, and the whole team rises and dips a little as it goes.
- THE EXISTING VILLAGE FIGURES. The existing skaters glide across the illustrated frozen pond holding hands, swaying and turning; the horse-drawn sleighs trot along the snowy banks with the horses' legs moving and their bells swinging. They continuously change position at real-time speed.
- THE GOLD LIGHT WAVES. Successive bright gold waves TRAVEL OUTWARD along the existing red-and-gold ribbon and evergreen-garland paths on the ceiling, each bright crest travelling about half of its ribbon's length every second, fading behind as the next bright crest follows, so the existing ribbons sparkle and flow continuously. No new rings or new objects appear; the moving light stays within the shapes that already exist in the image.
- THE LANTERNS AND THE PROJECTED ORNAMENT DRAWINGS - the golden lanterns flicker and sway within the flat wall illustration beside the cottages. Flat bauble and candy-cane drawings translate and turn along the wall and ceiling planes; they have no depth in the real room. Big gold bell drawings swing visibly side to side within the same projected surface, their clapper drawings moving inside them. All obey surface perspective and architectural occlusion.
- THE SNOWFLAKES AND THE POINTS OF LIGHT - these are strictly flat two-dimensional moving light marks on plaster, never physical flakes. On the ceiling they glide tangentially ALONG each ceiling surface toward its edge or wall seam, following its perspective; they never fall away from the ceiling into the room. On the walls they translate downward along the wall at about one quarter wall-height per second. At a wall-ceiling corner their path bends onto the adjoining surface and remains flush with it; behind the real boy, bed, headboard and beams they are occluded and disappear, never drawn over those objects. Keep their projected scale tied to the surface, with no foreground enlargement, blur, depth or free-space parallax. Every flake stays visibly attached to one architectural plane in every frame.
- THE SHOOTING STARS - the long-tailed shooting stars streak across, their tails thinning out behind them, and fade; fresh ones set off elsewhere.
- THE STAR FIELD - the small gold stars hold their places and twinkle, each brightening and dimming on its own timing, never all together.
- THE FULL MOON - the huge glowing full moon is the ONLY element that holds its centre position. It stays exactly where it is on the ceiling for the whole clip and only brightens and dims very slightly. Nothing else in the picture is allowed to hold still.
Because the wall and the whole ceiling are lit by the SAME projector, they are one picture and behave identically at the same instant. Whatever the flakes, the ribbons and the stars on the wall are doing, the flakes, the ribbons and the stars on the ceiling are doing at the same moment and at the same speed. There is never a still ceiling above a moving wall. Nothing is painted, printed or stencilled on the plaster.

TIMELINE - THE CAMERA MAKES EXACTLY TWO MOVES AND NO MORE, and they are the opposite of the other clips in this set: this one starts high and comes DOWN.
0.0-2.2s the camera is aimed HIGH at the start, weighted to the top of the wall shown in the starting frame and the beamed ceiling so the projected picture fills most of the frame and THE BOY is small and low near the bottom edge. From 0.0s the camera TILTS DOWN in one slow steady continuous move, the ribbons rippling, the travelling gold light waves spreading and the sleigh flying across the beamed ceiling as it goes, until the bed and THE BOY fill the lower half. THE BOY bends his knees and pushes off the mattress; both feet leave the quilt, arms swinging up over his head, mouth open laughing. The mattress compresses and springs back.
2.2-5.0s the tilt ends and the camera PULLS BACK a little in one slow steady move, the frame opening slightly wider so a little more of the bedroom comes in at the edges - a small gentle zoom out, nothing sudden. THE BOY lands on both feet and drives straight into a second, higher jump, knees tucking, then a third; he never stops moving and the body motion carries straight across the seam between the two camera moves.

AUDIO: room tone, mattress springs compressing on each landing, bedding rustling, a boy laughing. (no BGM). No subtitles.

POSITIVE LOCKS: real-time motion at normal speed - a real boy jumping at real speed and landing with real weight. Exactly one child. One continuous take with no cut. The camera makes exactly two moves: a slow tilt DOWN, then a small gentle pull back. The picture's outer boundary holds still while every element inside it moves. Wall and ceiling always move together. The picture stays sharp to the last frame.

DO NOT INCLUDE: blank or partly blank walls or ceiling at the start; a projection appearing, switching on, fading in, spreading, growing or revealing itself during the shot; an opening before-and-after transformation; a camera that tilts UP; a camera that pushes in; more than two camera moves; a hard or fast zoom; a frozen or still pattern; chimneys that are not smoking; a ribbon that sits still; a sleigh and reindeer parked in one place; skaters and horse-drawn sleighs that do not travel, or northern lights that do not ripple; travelling gold light waves that do not spread outward; flakes that only twinkle without travelling; a still ceiling above a moving wall; the whole picture rotating, spinning, sweeping or sliding as one sheet; slow motion; floating or weightless movement; any music; the projector unit in shot; a second child; an adult; blur; a cut; on-screen text; subtitles.

CONTINUOUS MOTION CHECK - from the first visible frame through the very last frame, the wall AND ceiling each contain clearly travelling projected elements at the same time: the sleigh changes position across the ceiling, its reindeer gallop; the existing illustrated skaters glide across the pond and the horse-drawn sleighs advance along the wall; lanterns flicker; the northern lights ripple; bells swing; ribbons carry travelling light waves; snowflakes translate across the surfaces. Twinkle alone and camera movement alone do not count. Maintain that same lively real-time motion with no opening freeze, no settling into a still image and no final freeze. All light bends with the real wall and ceiling geometry and stays on those surfaces.

SURFACE MAPPING - The five whitewashed timber beams and the flat ceiling strips between them remain stationary. Drawings split across beam faces and undersides with correct offsets and narrow shadows; the wooden French window frames and glazing bars carry the moving picture across their faces. All physical architecture stays stationary.

CURRENT BRIEF — STRONG CONTINUOUS MOTION: The existing skaters glide steadily across the illustrated frozen pond on the walls, holding hands, swaying and turning; the horse-drawn sleighs trot along the snowy banks with the horses' legs moving and their brass bells swinging; the snowmen's red scarves flutter and the golden lanterns flicker. Preserve the artwork already visible. Every illustrated chimney emits fresh curling smoke continuously, moving at least one chimney height per second; the sleigh crosses a substantial part of the ceiling with galloping reindeer. Existing ribbon light fields carry clearly travelling aurora-like waves along their length, with brightness crests moving rather than blinking in place. The existing northern-lights ring on the ceiling is ALWAYS in motion: its emerald and rose curtains ripple, fold and travel along their length like a real aurora, shimmering continuously from the first frame to the last, never a still painted band. The cottage structures and the full moon stay anchored while characters, vehicles, smoke and light waves travel independently. The only physical motion in the room is the specified children and camera; all Christmas artwork and illustrated smoke remain flat light on real wall/ceiling surfaces. No floating 3D objects. No frozen opening or ending. Use the exact architecture and artwork in this shot's references; animate only illustrated characters actually present, never invent source-template deer or rabbits. Natural room audio only, no music.
````

````text
Recreate @Image 1's camera, framing, scale and poses. Wholesome family Christmas scene. @Image 1 owns composition geometry only. @Image 2 owns the same boy's identity, Christmas pyjamas and dense saturated photographic projection look for this video; place that boy in the different bedroom described below. KEEP IDENTICAL: the same vertical 9:16 iPhone photo look, the same camera position and camera height, the same framing that shows the wall behind the bed and the ceiling above it both in shot, and the same natural low-key evening light in which the only light in the room is the projection itself.

CHANGE 1 - THE CHILD. A different child from @Image 1: a boy of about seven with fair skin, light freckles and tousled strawberry-blond curly hair, fully dressed for winter in a soft sky-blue brushed-cotton Christmas pyjama set patterned with small white polar bears in red scarves, green fir trees and silver snowflakes, with matching loose pyjama trousers and white wool socks. He is standing up on top of the bed, knees bent and both arms raised out to his sides, caught mid-bounce, head tipped back looking up at the projected ceiling, laughing. Clearly standing on top of the bed, not sitting on its edge.

CHANGE 2 - THE ROOM. Build a structurally believable room: a bright-proportioned Gustavian Scandinavian bedroom with a high BEAMED CEILING: five evenly spaced square-section whitewashed timber beams running from the back wall to the front across a flat white plaster ceiling. A white-painted Gustavian bed with a tall curved panelled headboard is centred on the flat back wall between two tall French windows, ivory bedding with a sage-green knitted throw, pale grey painted bedside chests, whitewashed oak floorboards and a soft sage-green wool rug. The bed stays in the same place in the frame, leaving the room's ceiling geometry clearly visible.

It is night, all house lamps switched off. The bed keeps the bedding described above, with pillows and a knitted stocking. A small real Christmas tree with gold lights, red ornaments and straw stars stands by the window.

CHANGE 3 - THE PROJECTION. ONE continuous Christmas panorama covers every visible wall and the entire ceiling, including the whole ceiling, every roof plane and seam, wall above the headboard, window jambs and lintels. No rectangle, no screen, no blank ceiling or wall patch. Architectural occlusion shadows remain real and narrow. LOW ALONG THE WALLS, a magical CHRISTMAS SKATING POND VILLAGE: a wide frozen pond glitters under golden lantern light while children in red scarves and bobble hats skate hand in hand, a tall decorated Christmas tree glows on a small snowy island at its centre, snowmen in red scarves line the banks, horse-drawn sleighs with brass bells pass cosy half-timbered cottages with warmly lit windows and thick snowy roofs, and tall snow-laden fir trees rise between them. Along the upper walls, strings of glowing golden lanterns, red velvet bows and dense emerald garlands link the cottage roofs into a continuous Christmas panorama. ACROSS THE CEILING, a great ring of emerald and rose northern-lights ribbons circles a huge glowing full moon, and Santa's red sleigh and eight reindeer cross the face of the moon in gold, their long curling red-and-gold ribbon trail woven among crisp snowflakes and tiny silver stars. Unmistakably Christmas Eve, glowing gold, cranberry red and icy sapphire blue dominant with evergreen accents against a near-black midnight sky. One dense continuous panorama of projected light across the architecture, no isolated sticker icons, no carnival, no galaxy, no vortex. No projected shape floats physically in the room. AND THE NIGHT SKY BETWEEN THE ELEMENTS IS DEEP AND DARK: everywhere the picture is not occupied by a lit element it is a rich near-black midnight sky of blackened indigo and very dark crimson, NOT white paint, NOT pale drywall and NOT a grey haze. It is that darkness between the elements that makes the bright ones burn.  HOW THE LIGHT SITS ON THE SURFACES, and this has to be exactly right. THE PROJECTION IS DENSE AND ROUGHLY NINETY PERCENT OPAQUE, and it is the brightest and most saturated thing in the frame. It lies on the room like an enormous backlit stained-glass window: deep, dense, fully saturated colour at full strength, crimson and ruby and warm gold and emerald at their richest, and it OVERPOWERS the colour of the surface underneath completely.  The material colour is overpowered by the projected colour; only surface texture and physical shadows remain legible through it. It is NOT pale, NOT pastel, NOT milky, NOT hazy, NOT semi-transparent, NOT a faint tint and NOT a washed-out overlay; there is no grey veil over any of it, and no part of the projection is so faint that the bare surface shows through as white.  AND IT WRAPS THE ARCHITECTURE. ONE SINGLE PROJECTOR sitting low in the near corner of the room throws all of this from that one point, so the entire picture keystones away from that origin and every surface takes it at its own angle. This is real projected light crossing a real three-dimensional room, so it obeys every surface it lands on.   The five timber beams stand clearly proud of the flat ceiling. The northern-lights ribbons and the sleigh split at every beam edge, run down each beam's vertical sides and across its underside, and resume on each flat ceiling strip between the beams at its actual depth. The flat wall and the white-painted wooden French window frames, glazing bars and casings carry properly foreshortened portions of the same continuous projection across their faces. All raised trim casts a real hard-edged shadow behind it. THE WOODEN FRAMES CARRY THE PICTURE TOO: the painted wooden window frames, sashes and glazing bars, the door and doorway casings, the skirting boards, the mantel and every other piece of wooden trim the beam crosses are covered by the same continuous projected imagery as the wall beside them - never left as bare white unlit outlines - with only a thin real shadow line at each edge. There is NO falloff anywhere. The picture stays strong, dense and fully coloured right into all four corners of the frame and right up into the corners of the ceiling, with no dim zone, no dark gap, no faded edge and no bare patch at any boundary of the room.  EVERY ELEMENT OF THE PROJECTION IS RAZOR SHARP: every elf, toy, ribbon, nutcracker detail, antler, snowflake, fir needle and window has precise edges. The thousands of points of light are DISCRETE PINPOINTS - tiny, tight, individually separated dots - and not soft round blobs, not fuzzy balls and not smeared streaks. There is NO bloom, NO glow halo, NO lens flare, NO atmospheric haze, NO soft-focus glaze and NO gaussian softness anywhere over the picture, and the brightest cores stay tight and do not bleed into what surrounds them. The whole frame is in focus from the nearest person to the farthest corner of the ceiling: nothing is out of focus, nothing is soft, and there is no depth-of-field blur and no bokeh anywhere in the image.

CHANGE 4 - NOTHING IS PROJECTED ON THE FLOOR. The projected imagery lives on the WALLS and the CEILING only. The timber floorboards, the soft sage-green wool rug, the bed and its covers, the bench at its foot and the little Christmas tree carry NO projected shapes at all - no snowflakes, no stars, no village, no Christmas figures, no gift village, no star, no sleigh, no falling light on them - they are lit only by the soft warm colour spill coming off the walls and sit in gentle shadow. The boy's face and his cotton pyjama top catch that spill so his face reads clearly.

CHANGE 5 - the projector unit must NOT be visible. No projector, no stool, no side table, no glowing device anywhere in frame. The corner it would occupy is plain wall and floor.

NATURAL, LESS CINEMATIC LOOK - a little less cinematic than a film still: this is a candid photo a parent snapped on an iPhone at home, not a movie frame, a poster or an advert. True-to-life colour and contrast as they come straight out of the phone camera: no colour grading, no teal-and-orange grade, no vignette, no dramatic spotlighting and no heightened fantasy glow on the children. The projection is still dense, bright and colourful, but it reads as the real light of a home Christmas projector falling on real walls, and the shadows keep a little ordinary room detail.

Vertical 9:16 photo, shot on an iPhone 16 Pro, natural handheld UGC look, extremely high quality, RAZOR SHARP EDGE TO EDGE AND CORNER TO CORNER at full 2K resolution with fine micro-detail legible everywhere - the fine cotton weave of the pyjamas, the grain of the floorboards, the tooth of the plaster, the crisp architectural edges of the ceiling and every individual pinpoint of the projection all crisply resolved - perfect lighting, perfectly exposed, hyper realistic, zero motion blur, zero depth-of-field blur, zero bokeh, zero bloom, zero glow haze, zero soft focus, nothing blurry, nothing smeared and nothing soft or gauzy anywhere in the frame, no CGI look. Normal wide-angle lens, straight vertical lines, no fisheye distortion.

No text, no captions, no watermarks, no lettering anywhere in the image.
````

````text
- CHIMNEY SMOKE: tall soft white smoke plumes stream continuously out of EVERY cottage chimney in the village, rising in long wavy columns up the wall, swaying and curling, each puff climbing about one roof height every second, widening and fading as fresh puffs push up from the chimney below. - LIGHTS: bright gold sparkle waves travel along the garlands and ribbons; stars twinkle; now and then a shooting star streaks across the ceiling.
````

### Image prompt
````text
Recreate @Image 1's camera, framing, scale and poses. Wholesome family Christmas scene. @Image 1 owns composition geometry only. @Image 2 owns the dense saturated photographic projection look and the identity of the new room and children; it is the same house viewed from farther inside. Both reference images are generated stills, not photographs of a documented real house.

KEEP IDENTICAL: the same vertical 9:16 iPhone photo look, the same camera position and camera height, the same wide framing that shows a whole enormous double-height room with its full ceiling in shot, the same magical low-key evening light in which the only light in the room is the projection itself, and the same two poses - a girl JUMPING ON THE SOFA, caught mid-air above the cushions, knees bent, arms flung up, hair lifted, the cushion compressed under where she pushed off; and a boy standing on the rug in front of the sofa, seen from behind and slightly to the side, head tipped back looking up at the projected ceiling with his arms out in wonder. Both are small in frame, in scale with the huge room - keeping them small preserves the sense of how big the space is.

CHANGE 1 - THE CHILDREN. The same new children as @Image 2: a girl of about five with light-brown skin and dark curly hair in two puffs tied with green velvet ribbons; a boy of about seven with fair skin, light freckles and tousled strawberry-blond curly hair. Both fully dressed in soft sky-blue brushed-cotton Christmas pyjama sets patterned with small white polar bears in red scarves, green fir trees and silver snowflakes, with matching loose pyjama trousers and white wool socks. Wholesome family Christmas scene.

CHANGE 2 - THE ROOM. Build a structurally believable room: a spacious double-height Gustavian Scandinavian Christmas great room with a high SHALLOW DOME CEILING: a broad smooth plaster saucer dome rising from a crisp circular stepped cornice ring, carried on four curved triangular corner pendentives over the square room. A tall white-painted panelled chimney breast stands on the back wall with a pale grey limestone mantel; two tall French windows with deep panelled reveals occupy the left wall and a wide white-trimmed round-arched opening sits on the right. Whitewashed oak floorboards, a large oatmeal bouclé sectional sofa, a round oatmeal upholstered ottoman and a soft sage-green wool rug. The same room and decor as @Image 2, seen from the middle of the great room with the whole ceiling in shot.

It is night, all house lamps switched off. A real Christmas tree with warm gold lights, red and silver baubles, woven straw stars, white velvet ribbon and wrapped gifts stands beside the fireplace. Evergreen garland with holly berries covers the mantel, with three red-and-white knitted stockings below. The projection is the main illumination.

CHANGE 3 - THE PROJECTION. ONE continuous Christmas panorama covers every visible wall and the entire ceiling, including the whole ceiling, every architectural plane and seam, every beam or cove, and the entire chimney breast all the way to the ceiling. No rectangle, no screen, no blank ceiling or wall patch. Architectural occlusion shadows remain real and narrow. LOW ALONG THE WALLS, a magical CHRISTMAS SKATING POND VILLAGE: a wide frozen pond glitters under golden lantern light while children in red scarves and bobble hats skate hand in hand, a tall decorated Christmas tree glows on a small snowy island at its centre, snowmen in red scarves line the banks, horse-drawn sleighs with brass bells pass cosy half-timbered cottages with warmly lit windows and thick snowy roofs, and tall snow-laden fir trees rise between them. Along the upper walls, strings of glowing golden lanterns, red velvet bows and dense emerald garlands link the cottage roofs into a continuous Christmas panorama. ACROSS THE CEILING, a great ring of emerald and rose northern-lights ribbons circles a huge glowing full moon, and Santa's red sleigh and eight reindeer cross the face of the moon in gold, their long curling red-and-gold ribbon trail woven among crisp snowflakes and tiny silver stars. Unmistakably Christmas Eve, glowing gold, cranberry red and icy sapphire blue dominant with evergreen accents against a near-black midnight sky. One dense continuous panorama of projected light across the architecture, no isolated sticker icons, no carnival, no galaxy, no vortex. No projected shape floats physically in the room. AND THE NIGHT SKY BETWEEN THE ELEMENTS IS DEEP AND DARK: everywhere the picture is not occupied by a lit element it is a rich near-black midnight sky of blackened indigo and very dark crimson, NOT white paint, NOT pale drywall and NOT a grey haze. It is that darkness between the elements that makes the bright ones burn.  HOW THE LIGHT SITS ON THE SURFACES, and this has to be exactly right. THE PROJECTION IS DENSE AND ROUGHLY NINETY PERCENT OPAQUE, and it is the brightest and most saturated thing in the frame. It lies on the room like an enormous backlit stained-glass window: deep, dense, fully saturated colour at full strength, crimson and ruby and warm gold and emerald at their richest, and it OVERPOWERS the colour of the surface underneath completely.  The material colour is overpowered by the projected colour; only surface texture and physical shadows remain legible through it. It is NOT pale, NOT pastel, NOT milky, NOT hazy, NOT semi-transparent, NOT a faint tint and NOT a washed-out overlay; there is no grey veil over any of it, and no part of the projection is so faint that the bare surface shows through as white.  AND IT WRAPS THE ARCHITECTURE. ONE SINGLE PROJECTOR sitting low in the near corner of the room throws all of this from that one point, so the entire picture keystones away from that origin and every surface takes it at its own angle. This is real projected light crossing a real three-dimensional room, so it obeys every surface it lands on.   The shallow dome, its circular cornice ring and the four curved pendentives remain clearly three-dimensional. The sleigh and the northern-lights ring curve with the dome, bend across the cornice ring and stretch down the curved pendentives before continuing onto the walls. The tall panelled chimney breast receives a continuous picture with tiny shadow breaks along its raised panel mouldings and clear folds at its two projecting corners. French window reveals and the arched opening's trim interrupt the light at their actual edges. Bright imagery follows the built curves and planes, never a flat picture hovering across the room. All raised trim casts a real hard-edged shadow behind it. There is NO falloff anywhere. The picture stays strong, dense and fully coloured right into all four corners of the frame and right up into the corners of the ceiling, with no dim zone, no dark gap, no faded edge and no bare patch at any boundary of the room.  EVERY ELEMENT OF THE PROJECTION IS RAZOR SHARP: every elf, toy, ribbon, nutcracker detail, antler, snowflake, fir needle and window has precise edges. The thousands of points of light are DISCRETE PINPOINTS - tiny, tight, individually separated dots - and not soft round blobs, not fuzzy balls and not smeared streaks. There is NO bloom, NO glow halo, NO lens flare, NO atmospheric haze, NO soft-focus glaze and NO gaussian softness anywhere over the picture, and the brightest cores stay tight and do not bleed into what surrounds them. Both fully dressed in soft sky-blue brushed-cotton Christmas pyjama sets patterned with small white polar bears in red scarves, green fir trees and silver snowflakes, with matching loose pyjama trousers and white wool socks. Wholesome family Christmas scene.
````

````text
CHANGE 2 - THE ROOM. Build a structurally believable room: a spacious double-height Gustavian Scandinavian Christmas great room with a high SHALLOW DOME CEILING: a broad smooth plaster saucer dome rising from a crisp circular stepped cornice ring, carried on four curved triangular corner pendentives over the square room. A tall white-painted panelled chimney breast stands on the back wall with a pale grey limestone mantel; two tall French windows with deep panelled reveals occupy the left wall and a wide white-trimmed round-arched opening sits on the right. Whitewashed oak floorboards, a large oatmeal bouclé sectional sofa, a round oatmeal upholstered ottoman and a soft sage-green wool rug. The same room and decor as @Image 2, seen from the middle of the great room with the whole ceiling in shot.

It is night, all house lamps switched off. A real Christmas tree with warm gold lights, red and silver baubles, woven straw stars, white velvet ribbon and wrapped gifts stands beside the fireplace. Evergreen garland with holly berries covers the mantel, with three red-and-white knitted stockings below. The projection is the main illumination.

CHANGE 3 - THE PROJECTION. ONE continuous Christmas panorama covers every visible wall and the entire ceiling, including the whole ceiling, every architectural plane and seam, every beam or cove, and the entire chimney breast all the way to the ceiling. No rectangle, no screen, no blank ceiling or wall patch. Architectural occlusion shadows remain real and narrow. LOW ALONG THE WALLS, a magical CHRISTMAS SKATING POND VILLAGE: a wide frozen pond glitters under golden lantern light while children in red scarves and bobble hats skate hand in hand, a tall decorated Christmas tree glows on a small snowy island at its centre, snowmen in red scarves line the banks, horse-drawn sleighs with brass bells pass cosy half-timbered cottages with warmly lit windows and thick snowy roofs, and tall snow-laden fir trees rise between them. Along the upper walls, strings of glowing golden lanterns, red velvet bows and dense emerald garlands link the cottage roofs into a continuous Christmas panorama. ACROSS THE CEILING, a great ring of emerald and rose northern-lights ribbons circles a huge glowing full moon, and Santa's red sleigh and eight reindeer cross the face of the moon in gold, their long curling red-and-gold ribbon trail woven among crisp snowflakes and tiny silver stars. Unmistakably Christmas Eve, glowing gold, cranberry red and icy sapphire blue dominant with evergreen accents against a near-black midnight sky. One dense continuous panorama of projected light across the architecture, no isolated sticker icons, no carnival, no galaxy, no vortex. No projected shape floats physically in the room. AND THE NIGHT SKY BETWEEN THE ELEMENTS IS DEEP AND DARK: everywhere the picture is not occupied by a lit element it is a rich near-black midnight sky of blackened indigo and very dark crimson, NOT white paint, NOT pale drywall and NOT a grey haze. It is that darkness between the elements that makes the bright ones burn.  HOW THE LIGHT SITS ON THE SURFACES, and this has to be exactly right. THE PROJECTION IS DENSE AND ROUGHLY NINETY PERCENT OPAQUE, and it is the brightest and most saturated thing in the frame. It lies on the room like an enormous backlit stained-glass window: deep, dense, fully saturated colour at full strength, crimson and ruby and warm gold and emerald at their richest, and it OVERPOWERS the colour of the surface underneath completely.  The material colour is overpowered by the projected colour; only surface texture and physical shadows remain legible through it. It is NOT pale, NOT pastel, NOT milky, NOT hazy, NOT semi-transparent, NOT a faint tint and NOT a washed-out overlay; there is no grey veil over any of it, and no part of the projection is so faint that the bare surface shows through as white.  AND IT WRAPS THE ARCHITECTURE. ONE SINGLE PROJECTOR sitting low in the near corner of the room throws all of this from that one point, so the entire picture keystones away from that origin and every surface takes it at its own angle. This is real projected light crossing a real three-dimensional room, so it obeys every surface it lands on.   The shallow dome, its circular cornice ring and the four curved pendentives remain clearly three-dimensional. The sleigh and the northern-lights ring curve with the dome, bend across the cornice ring and stretch down the curved pendentives before continuing onto the walls. The tall panelled chimney breast receives a continuous picture with tiny shadow breaks along its raised panel mouldings and clear folds at its two projecting corners. The painted wooden French window frames, glazing bars and reveals and the arched opening's wooden trim carry the projection straight across their faces, with thin shadow breaks only at their actual edges. Bright imagery follows the built curves and planes, never a flat picture hovering across the room. All raised trim casts a real hard-edged shadow behind it. THE WOODEN FRAMES MUST NOT READ AS WHITE - THIS IS THE MOST IMPORTANT FIX. Every window frame, sash, glazing bar and window reveal, the casing of the arched opening, the skirting boards, the mantel, the panelling and the full height of the chimney breast above the mantel, the headboard and every ceiling beam sit directly in the projector's beam, so the projected picture lands on them exactly as it lands on the wall: the same village, garlands, snowflakes and dark midnight sky continue unbroken across every frame member, cutting it into coloured pieces. The painted wood takes on the projected colours and the dark night-sky tone of the picture around it, so from the camera the frames almost disappear into the projection. NO bright white or cream window grids, NO clean white outlines, NO pale unlit trim, NO plain white column anywhere in the frame. The only clue to where a frame is: a thin shadow line along its edge and a slight shift in the picture where the surface steps in or out. The window glass itself stays dark night glass. There is NO falloff anywhere. The picture stays strong, dense and fully coloured right into all four corners of the frame and right up into the corners of the ceiling, with no dim zone, no dark gap, no faded edge and no bare patch at any boundary of the room.  EVERY ELEMENT OF THE PROJECTION IS RAZOR SHARP: every elf, toy, ribbon, nutcracker detail, antler, snowflake, fir needle and window has precise edges. The thousands of points of light are DISCRETE PINPOINTS - tiny, tight, individually separated dots - and not soft round blobs, not fuzzy balls and not smeared streaks. There is NO bloom, NO glow halo, NO lens flare, NO atmospheric haze, NO soft-focus glaze and NO gaussian softness anywhere over the picture, and the brightest cores stay tight and do not bleed into what surrounds them. The whole frame is in focus from the nearest person to the farthest corner of the ceiling: nothing is out of focus, nothing is soft, and there is no depth-of-field blur and no bokeh anywhere in the image.

CHANGE 4 - NOTHING IS PROJECTED ON THE FLOOR. The projected imagery lives on the WALLS and the CEILING only. The timber floorboards, the soft sage-green wool rug, the oatmeal bouclé sectional sofa, the round upholstered ottoman and the tree carry NO projected shapes at all - no snowflakes, no stars, no village, no Christmas figures, no gift village, no star, no sleigh, no falling light on them - they are lit only by the soft warm colour spill coming off the walls and sit in gentle shadow. Both children catch that spill so their cotton pyjamas read clearly.

CHANGE 5 - the projector unit must NOT be visible. No projector, no round side table, no glowing device anywhere in frame. The corner it would occupy is plain rug and floor.

NATURAL, LESS CINEMATIC LOOK - a little less cinematic than a film still: this is a candid photo a parent snapped on an iPhone at home, not a movie frame, a poster or an advert. True-to-life colour and contrast as they come straight out of the phone camera: no colour grading, no teal-and-orange grade, no vignette, no dramatic spotlighting and no heightened fantasy glow on the children. The projection is still dense, bright and colourful, but it reads as the real light of a home Christmas projector falling on real walls, and the shadows keep a little ordinary room detail.

Vertical 9:16 photo, shot on an iPhone 16 Pro, natural handheld UGC look, extremely high quality, RAZOR SHARP EDGE TO EDGE AND CORNER TO CORNER at full 2K resolution with fine micro-detail legible everywhere - the fine cotton weave of the pyjamas, the grain of the floorboards, the tooth of the drywall, the surface texture of the white-painted panelled plaster and every individual pinpoint of the projection all crisply resolved - perfect lighting, perfectly exposed, hyper realistic, zero motion blur, zero depth-of-field blur, zero bokeh, zero bloom, zero glow haze, zero soft focus, nothing blurry, nothing smeared and nothing soft or gauzy anywhere in the frame, no CGI look. Real home video, shot on a hand-held iPhone by a person standing in the room a few steps behind her, phone held upright at chest height. One continuous shot. The camera stays in exactly the same spot for the whole clip with only tiny natural hand sway and micro-jitter - the small live breathing wobble of real hand-held phone footage, never a steady locked-off tripod look. Her chest is still moving and she is still there when the clip ends.
````

````text
The inflatable Santa stays taped to the ceiling the whole time.

No slow motion. No speed ramp, no speed change of any kind - the clip plays at one constant real-time speed from start to finish. No zoom, no push-in, no pan, no tilt. No captions or text.
````

````text
- NORTHERN LIGHTS: the emerald and rose aurora curtains around the moon BILLOW AND SWEEP like real aurora filmed in time-lapse. Bright folds roll around the ring about one eighth of the circle every second, curtains stretch, sway, fold over and re-form, surges of brighter green and pink flow along them, and their shape is clearly different every second.
- SANTA'S SLEIGH AND EIGHT REINDEER: the team FLIES. It travels in one smooth sweeping arc across the ceiling, entering from where it sits in the room reference and flying across the face of the moon and on toward the far side of the dome, covering about one quarter of the ceiling's width every second, so it is in a clearly different place every second. The reindeer's legs gallop in a running cycle, heads bob, the sleigh rocks and dips, and the golden trail streams and curls behind it.
- CHIMNEY SMOKE: tall soft white smoke plumes stream continuously out of EVERY cottage chimney in the village, rising in long wavy columns up the wall, swaying and curling, each puff climbing about one roof height every second, widening and fading as fresh puffs push up from the chimney below. Several plumes are clearly rising at once, all the time.
- VILLAGE LIFE: the skaters glide in loops around the frozen pond holding hands; the horse-drawn sleighs trot along the banks with horses' legs moving; lantern flames flicker; the snowmen's scarves flap.
- SNOWFALL: big projected snowflakes drift steadily down across the whole picture, about a quarter of the wall height every second, turning as they fall, flat on the wall and ceiling surfaces.
- LIGHTS: bright gold sparkle waves travel along the garlands and ribbons; stars twinkle; now and then a shooting star streaks across the ceiling.
PER-SECOND ANIMATION TRACK (5 s):
0-1s: the door opens onto the room; through the gap the aurora is already billowing and smoke is already rising from the chimneys.
1-2s: the sleigh team is flying fast across the ceiling toward and across the moon, the reindeer galloping; smoke columns climb the wall; snow drifts down.
2-3s: the aurora folds roll visibly around the ring; the sleigh has moved on a quarter of the ceiling; skaters loop the pond.
3-4s: the sleigh is past the moon heading for the far side of the ceiling; new smoke puffs rise from every chimney; a shooting star streaks across.
4-5s: all of it still moving at the same pace in the final frame - aurora sweeping, sleigh flying, smoke rising, snow falling.
All of this is flat projected light lying on the real plaster, trim and window frames: it bends with the surfaces, never floats into the room, and nothing is projected on the floor, furniture or children.

CONTINUOUS ACTION, NOT CAMERA PHASES
0.0-5.0s - ONE identical slow forward-and-upward glide continues for the full duration, already underway at frame zero. The view rises gently while moving forward at the same steady ratio throughout. During approximately the first 1.5 seconds the hand turns the knob and opens the door with a smooth, ordinary unhurried push, never a quick shove or a fast reveal. The camera does not wait for the door, rush through it, pause for the room, change speed at the sill, or start a new movement afterward. The closed-door frame and the revealed room belong to the same continuous physical shot. The room behind it is already running AND BOTH CHILDREN ARE ALREADY IN MOTION - the widening gap only uncovers more of a movie that was already playing and two kids who were already running before the door opened. Nothing ignites or spreads as the door opens, and the door edge and frame stay plain painted wood - no glowing strip, no coloured fringe, no bright seam in the gap. Cross only the single thin threshold directly into the living room; no intervening space. There is no stop at the sofa or separate ceiling reveal. The shot ends during that same gentle glide, with back wall, furniture and ceiling still visible. All through it the smoke pours from every chimney, the northern lights ripple, the sleigh flies on across the ceiling, the existing illustrated skaters and horse-drawn sleighs keep moving, the lanterns flicker, the travelling gold light waves keep spreading and the snow keeps falling.
Inside that single move, the children are playing ONE CONTINUOUS GAME OF CHASE on the open floor. They are already mid-stride before the door reveals them. The girl, about five with light-brown skin and dark curly hair in two puffs tied with green velvet ribbons, runs a broad curving path across the rug and alongside the sofa; the boy, about seven with fair skin, light freckles and tousled strawberry-blond curly hair, follows two steps behind, both laughing, taking quick alternating footfalls with swinging arms. They continue the same open-ended curving chase naturally around the available floor space, curving away from furniture and each other; this is not a loop that returns them to a reference pose. During the final 1.5 seconds the girl continues around the right-hand edge of the rug while the boy follows two running strides behind, with their bodies at different depths and turned along their route. The girl can leave the frame at the side while still running and the boy keeps following; neither child is required to remain centred or fully visible. Their positions change in every successive instant, including the middle of the shot. They do not arrive at a destination, stop beside each other, face the camera, line up, switch activities or climb onto the sofa. This is already-running, still-running, keep-running: the same continuous playful action before the door opens, through the reveal and through the last frame in which they are visible. While running, the boy throws one arm up and points at the flying sleigh and the rippling northern lights on the ceiling, and the girl looks up in delight mid-stride, but their feet keep stepping and their momentum never stops. Real speed, grounded feet and natural weight; no sliding, teleporting or running in place.

CAMERA - eye height, normal wide-angle phone lens, a steady natural phone glide. One constant slow forward-and-upward movement, both components active together from frame zero through frame last, with the same gentle speed and same ratio throughout. No opening rush, acceleration, easing, speed ramp, phase change, level hold, delayed upward tilt, pitch jump, reframe, stop, second move, cut, concealed edit, dissolve, teleport, zoom, orbit, fisheye or lens warping. The camera is the person's eyes; no phone, screen or device in frame.

EXACTLY TWO CHILDREN, no adults, no third child, no pets. Hold the sharpness and exposure of the start image - the panel edges of the door, the brass knob, the forearm and sleeve exactly as shown in the starting image, with no jewellery. Crisp phone footage, in focus first frame to last.

AUDIO - dry documentary sound only: the handle, the door swinging, continuous alternating running footfalls, children laughing, quiet room tone. (no BGM). No subtitles.

REFERENCE-ONLY LOCK - @Image 2 is the existing still room reference. It supplies architecture, children's identities, clothing, furniture and artwork appearance ONLY. It is not an end frame, camera target or transition destination. Never dissolve, cut, morph or snap into its exact composition. Only the closed-door @Image 1 defines frame zero; animate the existing room content independently throughout the same uninterrupted physical entrance. They continuously run their playful chase at different depths, independent of those stationary poses. Never recreate the two children standing together in front of the sofa, at any point including the final frame. ROOM AND IDENTITY - a spacious double-height Gustavian Scandinavian Christmas great room with a high SHALLOW DOME CEILING: a broad smooth plaster saucer dome rising from a crisp circular stepped cornice ring, carried on four curved triangular corner pendentives over the square room. A tall white-painted panelled chimney breast stands on the back wall with a pale grey limestone mantel; two tall French windows with deep panelled reveals occupy the left wall and a wide white-trimmed round-arched opening sits on the right. Whitewashed oak floorboards, a large oatmeal bouclé sectional sofa, a round oatmeal upholstered ottoman and a soft sage-green wool rug. The side opening on the right wall remains lateral to the route, never an extra threshold ahead. a girl of about five with light-brown skin and dark curly hair in two puffs tied with green velvet ribbons; a boy of about seven with fair skin, light freckles and tousled strawberry-blond curly hair. Both fully dressed in soft sky-blue brushed-cotton Christmas pyjama sets patterned with small white polar bears in red scarves, green fir trees and silver snowflakes, with matching loose pyjama trousers and white wool socks. Preserve these identities and architecture from @Image 2.

PROJECTED SCENE CONTENT - LOW ALONG THE WALLS, a magical CHRISTMAS SKATING POND VILLAGE: a wide frozen pond glitters under golden lantern light while children in red scarves and bobble hats skate hand in hand, a tall decorated Christmas tree glows on a small snowy island at its centre, snowmen in red scarves line the banks, horse-drawn sleighs with brass bells pass cosy half-timbered cottages with warmly lit windows and thick snowy roofs, and tall snow-laden fir trees rise between them. Along the upper walls, strings of glowing golden lanterns, red velvet bows and dense emerald garlands link the cottage roofs into a continuous Christmas panorama. ACROSS THE CEILING, a great ring of emerald and rose northern-lights ribbons circles a huge glowing full moon, and Santa's red sleigh and eight reindeer cross the face of the moon in gold, their long curling red-and-gold ribbon trail woven among crisp snowflakes and tiny silver stars. Unmistakably Christmas Eve, glowing gold, cranberry red and icy sapphire blue dominant with evergreen accents against a near-black midnight sky. One dense continuous panorama of projected light across the architecture, no isolated sticker icons, no carnival, no galaxy, no vortex. No projected shape floats physically in the room.

BANNED - a camera that moves, stops and then moves again; two separate camera moves; a forward-only push followed by a late upward tilt; any camera acceleration or speed ramp; treating the room reference as a destination; either child climbing onto the sofa, standing still, posed, stopping before the last frame, or aligning side by side in front of the sofa; a glow or bright seam along the door edge; light that grows or fills the room as the door opens; the projector unit visible; blur; a cut; an adult.

MOTION GUARDRAILS - Failed if: the aurora looks like a still painted band; the sleigh stays in one place; any chimney has no rising smoke; snow does not fall; the picture moves only because the camera moves; the animation starts late, pauses or freezes at the end; the whole picture rotates or slides as one sheet; any projected shape floats in the room. Real-time speed, no slow motion, no music, no subtitles.
````

````text
The boy is of about seven with fair skin, light freckles and tousled strawberry-blond curly hair; the girl is of about five with light-brown skin and dark curly hair in two puffs tied with green velvet ribbons. Both children are fully dressed for winter in soft sky-blue brushed-cotton Christmas pyjama sets patterned with small white polar bears in red scarves, green fir trees and silver snowflakes, with matching loose pyjama trousers and white wool socks. Wholesome family Christmas scene.

CHANGE 1 - THE ROOM. Build a structurally believable room: a spacious double-height Gustavian Scandinavian Christmas great room with a high SHALLOW DOME CEILING: a broad smooth plaster saucer dome rising from a crisp circular stepped cornice ring, carried on four curved triangular corner pendentives over the square room. A tall white-painted panelled chimney breast stands on the back wall with a pale grey limestone mantel; two tall French windows with deep panelled reveals occupy the left wall and a wide white-trimmed round-arched opening sits on the right. Whitewashed oak floorboards, a large oatmeal bouclé sectional sofa, a round oatmeal upholstered ottoman and a soft sage-green wool rug. Seen from the doorway; the new a soft dove-grey painted solid wood door with two tall arched-top upper panels and two short square lower panels, a satin finish and crisp white-painted surrounding trim is open out of frame.

It is night, all house lamps switched off. A real Christmas tree with warm gold lights, red and silver baubles, woven straw stars, white velvet ribbon and wrapped gifts stands beside the fireplace. Evergreen garland with holly berries covers the mantel, with three red-and-white knitted stockings below. The projection is the main illumination.

CHANGE 2 - THE PROJECTION. ONE continuous Christmas panorama covers every visible wall and the entire ceiling, including the whole ceiling, every architectural plane and seam, every beam or cove, and the entire chimney breast all the way to the ceiling. No rectangle, no screen, no blank ceiling or wall patch. Architectural occlusion shadows remain real and narrow. LOW ALONG THE WALLS, a magical CHRISTMAS SKATING POND VILLAGE: a wide frozen pond glitters under golden lantern light while children in red scarves and bobble hats skate hand in hand, a tall decorated Christmas tree glows on a small snowy island at its centre, snowmen in red scarves line the banks, horse-drawn sleighs with brass bells pass cosy half-timbered cottages with warmly lit windows and thick snowy roofs, and tall snow-laden fir trees rise between them. Along the upper walls, strings of glowing golden lanterns, red velvet bows and dense emerald garlands link the cottage roofs into a continuous Christmas panorama. ACROSS THE CEILING, a great ring of emerald and rose northern-lights ribbons circles a huge glowing full moon, and Santa's red sleigh and eight reindeer cross the face of the moon in gold, their long curling red-and-gold ribbon trail woven among crisp snowflakes and tiny silver stars. Unmistakably Christmas Eve, glowing gold, cranberry red and icy sapphire blue dominant with evergreen accents against a near-black midnight sky. One dense continuous panorama of projected light across the architecture, no isolated sticker icons, no carnival, no galaxy, no vortex. No projected shape floats physically in the room. AND THE NIGHT SKY BETWEEN THE ELEMENTS IS DEEP AND DARK: everywhere the picture is not occupied by a lit element it is a rich near-black midnight sky of blackened indigo and very dark crimson, NOT white paint, NOT pale drywall and NOT a grey haze. It is that darkness between the elements that makes the bright ones burn.  HOW THE LIGHT SITS ON THE SURFACES, and this has to be exactly right. THE PROJECTION IS DENSE AND ROUGHLY NINETY PERCENT OPAQUE, and it is the brightest and most saturated thing in the frame. It lies on the room like an enormous backlit stained-glass window: deep, dense, fully saturated colour at full strength, crimson and ruby and warm gold and emerald at their richest, and it OVERPOWERS the colour of the surface underneath completely.  The material colour is overpowered by the projected colour; only surface texture and physical shadows remain legible through it. It is NOT pale, NOT pastel, NOT milky, NOT hazy, NOT semi-transparent, NOT a faint tint and NOT a washed-out overlay; there is no grey veil over any of it, and no part of the projection is so faint that the bare surface shows through as white.  AND IT WRAPS THE ARCHITECTURE. ONE SINGLE PROJECTOR sitting low in the near corner of the room throws all of this from that one point, so the entire picture keystones away from that origin and every surface takes it at its own angle. This is real projected light crossing a real three-dimensional room, so it obeys every surface it lands on.   The shallow dome, its circular cornice ring and the four curved pendentives remain clearly three-dimensional. The sleigh and the northern-lights ring curve with the dome, bend across the cornice ring and stretch down the curved pendentives before continuing onto the walls. The tall panelled chimney breast receives a continuous picture with tiny shadow breaks along its raised panel mouldings and clear folds at its two projecting corners. French window reveals and the arched opening's trim interrupt the light at their actual edges. Bright imagery follows the built curves and planes, never a flat picture hovering across the room. All raised trim casts a real hard-edged shadow behind it. There is NO falloff anywhere. The picture stays strong, dense and fully coloured right into all four corners of the frame and right up into the corners of the ceiling, with no dim zone, no dark gap, no faded edge and no bare patch at any boundary of the room.  EVERY ELEMENT OF THE PROJECTION IS RAZOR SHARP: every elf, toy, ribbon, nutcracker detail, antler, snowflake, fir needle and window has precise edges. The thousands of points of light are DISCRETE PINPOINTS - tiny, tight, individually separated dots - and not soft round blobs, not fuzzy balls and not smeared streaks. There is NO bloom, NO glow halo, NO lens flare, NO atmospheric haze, NO soft-focus glaze and NO gaussian softness anywhere over the picture, and the brightest cores stay tight and do not bleed into what surrounds them. The whole frame is in focus from the nearest person to the farthest corner of the ceiling: nothing is out of focus, nothing is soft, and there is no depth-of-field blur and no bokeh anywhere in the image.

CHANGE 3 - NOTHING IS PROJECTED ON THE FLOOR. The projected imagery lives on the WALLS and the CEILING only. The timber floorboards, the soft sage-green wool rug, the oatmeal bouclé sectional sofa, the round upholstered ottoman, the tree and the gifts carry NO projected shapes at all - no snowflakes, no stars, no village, no Christmas figures, no gift village, no star, no sleigh, no falling light on them - they are lit only by the soft warm colour spill coming off the walls and sit in gentle shadow. Both children's faces and their cotton pyjamas catch that spill so both faces read clearly.

CHANGE 4 - the projector unit must NOT be visible. No projector, no side table, no stool, no glowing device anywhere in frame. The corner it would occupy is plain floor and the edge of the rug.
````
